data[\\\"true_params\\\"][\\\"theta\\\"]\\n        theta_true_c = theta_true - theta_true.mean()\\n        c_true = data[\\\"true_params\\\"][\\\"c\\\"]\\n        e_values = data[\\\"true_params\\\"][\\\"e\\\"]\\n        seq = fit_sequential(data, e_values)\\n        bias = seq[\\\"theta_hat\\\"] - theta_true_c\\n        all_c_true.extend(c_true.tolist())\\n        all_bias.extend(bias.tolist())\\n    return np.array(all_c_true), np.array(all_bias)\\n\\n\\ndef plot_fig2_ovb_bias():\\n    print(\\\"Generating fig2_ovb_bias...\\\")\\n    all_c, all_bias = generate_ovb_scatter_data()\\n    r, p = sp_stats.pearsonr(all_c, all_bias)\\n    print(f\\\"  Pearson r={r:.4f}, p={p:.6f}\\\")\\n\\n    fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=STYLE[\\\"figsize_single\\\"])\\n    ax.scatter(all_c, all_bias, alpha=0.35, s=12, c=COLORS[\\\"sequential\\\"],\\n               edgecolors=\\\"none\\\", zorder=2)\\n    ax.axhline(0, color=\\\"gray\\\", linestyle=\\\"--\\\", linewidth=0.8, alpha=0.6)\\n\\n    z = np.polyfit(all_c, all_bias, 1)\\n    x_line = np.linspace(all_c.min(), all_c.max(), 100)\\n    ax.plot(x_line, np.polyval(z, x_line), color=\\\"#222222\\\", linewidth=1.8,\\n            label=f\\\"$r = {r:.2f}$\\\", zorder=3)\\n\\n    ax.set_xlabel(r\\\"$c_n$ (true contamination susceptibility)\\\")\\n    ax.set_ylabel(r\\\"$\\\\hat{\\\\theta}_{\\\\mathrm{seq}} - \\\\theta_{\\\\mathrm{true}}$\\\")\\n    ax.legend(loc=\\\"upper left\\\", framealpha=0.9)\\n    add_grid(ax)\\n    fig.tight_layout()\\n    save_fig(fig, \\\"fig2_ovb_bias\\\")\\n    plt.close(fig)\\n\\n\\n# ════════��══════════════ Figure 4: Bias vs Rho ═══════════════════════\\n\\ndef plot_fig4_bias_vs_rho():\\n    print(\\\"Generating fig4_bias_vs_rho...\\\")\\n    csv_path = \\\"/root/gated_mfrm_judge_bias/exp009/bias_vs_rho.csv\\\"\\n    df = pd.read_csv(csv_path)\\n\\n    fig, axes = plt.subplots(1, 3, figsize=STYLE[\\\"figsize_triple\\\"])\\n\\n    metrics = [\\n        (\\\"bias_slope\\\", r\\\"Bias slope $\\\\beta_1(\\\\hat{\\\\theta}-\\\\theta \\\\sim c_n)$\\\",\\n         \\\"Bias Slope\\\"),\\n        (\\\"rmse\\\", r\\\"RMSE ($\\\\theta$ recovery)\\\", \\\"RMSE\\\"),\\n        (\\\"kendall_tau\\\", r\\\"Kendall $\\\\tau$\\\", \\\"Ranking Accuracy\\\"),\\n    ]\\n\\n    for ax, (col, ylabel, title) in zip(axes, metrics):\\n        for method in [\\\"sequential\\\", \\\"joint\\\"]:\\n            sub = df[df[\\\"method\\\"] == method]\\n            g = sub.groupby(\\\"rho\\\")\\n            x = g[col].mean().index.values\\n            y = g[col].mean().values\\n            ci = 1.96 * g[col].std().values / np.sqrt(g.size().values)\\n            ax.plot(x, y, marker=MARKERS[method], color=COLORS[method],\\n                    label=method.capitalize(), linewidth=1.8, markersize=5)\\n            ax.fill_between(x, y - ci, y + ci, color=COLORS[method], alpha=0.12)\\n\\n        if col == \\\"bias_slope\\\":\\n            ax.axhline(0, color=\\\"gray\\\", ls=\\\"--\\\", alpha=0.5, linewidth=0.8)\\n\\n        ax.set_xlabel(r\\\"Confounding strength ($\\\\rho$)\\\")\\n        ax.set_ylabel(ylabel)\\n        ax.legend(framealpha=0.9)\\n        add_grid(ax)\\n\\n    fig.tight_layout()\\n    save_fig(fig, \\\"fig4_bias_vs_rho\\\")\\n    plt.close(fig)\\n\\n\\n# ═══════════════════════ Main ══���════════════════════\\n\\nif __name__ == \\\"__main__\\\":\\n    apply_style()\\n    os.makedirs(OUTPUT_DIR, exist_ok=True)\\n    plot_fig2_ovb_bias()\\n    plot_fig4_bias_vs_rho()\\n    print(\\\"\\\\nDone. All figures saved to:\\\", OUTPUT_DIR)\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.52}}"}}}

with -d at strength rho.\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)\\\\n\\\\n    theta = rng.standard_normal(N)\\\\n    b = rng.standard_normal(I)\\\\n    d = rng.normal(0, 0.5, size=J)\\\\n    c = np.abs(rng.normal(0, 1.5, size=N))\\\\n\\\\n    # Bimodal exposure: 30% high, 70% low\\\\n    n_high = int(I * 0.3)\\\\n    e = np.zeros(I)\\\\n    idx = rng.permutation(I)\\\\n    e[idx[:n_high]] = rng.uniform(0.7, 1.0, size=n_high)\\\\n    e[idx[n_high:]] = rng.uniform(0.0, 0.2, size=I - n_high)\\\\n\\\\n    # Judge contamination sensitivity correlated with -d_j\\\\n    d_std = d.std()\\\\n    if d_std < 1e-8:\\\\n        d_std = 1.0\\\\n    z_s = rng.standard_normal(J)\\\\n    s = 0.8 * (-rho * (d / d_std) + np.sqrt(1 - rho ** 2) * probs = expit(logits)\\\\n    responses = rng.binomial(1, probs).astype(np.int8)\\\\n\\\\n    return {\\\\n        \\\\\\\"responses\\\\\\\": responses,\\\\n        \\\\\\\"true_params\\\\\\\": Observation\\n\\nPart A: OVB Monte Carlo — measures sequential estimator bias as rho varies.\\n        rho in {0, 0.3, 0.6, 0.9} x 50 seeds, records signed bias + RMSE.\\nPart B: c_n ground-truth recovery — 95% credible interval coverage under\\n        joint G-MFRM at rho=0.6, 50 seeds.\\n\\nInputs:  ../mvp/{models.py, models_v3.py}\\nOutputs: bias_vs_rho.csv, bias_vs_rho.png, cn_recovery.csv, cn_recovery_scatter.png\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\nimport os\\n\\nos.environ[\\\"XLA_FLAGS\\\"] = \\\"--xla_force_host_platform_device_count=4\\\"\\n\\nimport sys\\nimport time\\nimport argparse\\n\\nimport numpy as np\\nimport pandas as pd\\nimport matplotlib\\nmatplotlib.use(\\\"Agg\\\")\\nimport matplotlib.pyplot as plt\\nfrom scipy.stats import kendalltau\\nfrom scipy.special import expit\\n\\nsys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)), \\\"..\\\", \\\"mvp\\\"))\\n\\nimport jax\\nimport jax.numpy as jnp\\nimport numpyro\\nimport numpyro.distributions as dist\\nfrom numpyro.infer import MCMC, NUTS\\n\\nfrom models_v3 import fit_sequential_v3, fit_joint_gmfrm_v3, _gmfrm_model_v3\\nfrom models import _mcmc_diagnostics\\n\\nOUTPUT_DIR = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))\\n\\n\\n# ═══════════════════════ DGP (MVP-002 with parameterized rho) ═══════════════════════\\n\\ndef generate_exp009_data(N=50, I=100, J=8, rho=0.3, seed=42):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"MVP-002 DGP with variable rho.\\n\\n    c ~ HalfNormal(1.5), bimodal e (30% high / 70% low),\\n    judge sensitivity s ~ N(0, 0.8) correlated with -d at strength rho.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)\\n\\n    theta = rng.standard_normal(N)\\n    b = rng.standard_normal(I)\\n    d = rng.normal(0, 0.5, size=J)\\n    c = np.abs(rng.normal(0, 1.5, size=N))\\n\\n    # Bimodal exposure: 30% high, 70% low\\n    n_high = int(I * 0.3)\\n    e = np.zeros(I)\\n    idx = rng.permutation(I)\\n    e[idx[:n_high]] = rng.uniform(0.7, 1.0, size=n_high)\\n    e[idx[n_high:]] = rng.uniform(0.0, 0.2, size=I - n_high)\\n\\n    # Judge contamination sensitivity correlated with -d_j\\n    d_std = d.std()\\n    if d_std < 1e-8:\\n        d_std = 1.0\\n    z_s = rng.standard_normal(J)\\n    s = 0.8 * (-rho * (d / d_std) + np.sqrt(1 - rho ** 2) * z_s)\\n\\n    contamination = c[:, None, None] * e[None, :, None] * (1.0 + s[None, None, :])\\n    logits = theta[:, None, None] - b[None, :, None] - d[None, None, :] + contamination\\n    probs = expit(logits)\\n    responses = rng.binomial(1, probs).astype(np.int8)\\n\\n    return {\\n        \\\"responses\\\": responses,\\n        \\\"true_params\\\": {\\\"theta\\\": theta, \\\"b\\\": b, \\\"d\\\": d, \\\"c\\\": c, \\\"e\\\": e, \\\"s\\\": s},\\n        \\\"config\\\": {\\\"N\\\": N, \\\"I\\\": I, \\\"J\\\": J, \\\"rho\\\": rho, \\\"seed\\\": seed},\\n    }\\n\\n\\n# ═══════════════════════ Joint G-MFRM returning full c posterior ═══════════════════════\\n\\ndef fit_joint_gmfrm_v3_full(data, e_values):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Like fit_joint_gmfrm_v3 but returns full c posterior samples for CI.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    cfg = data[\\\"config\\\"]\\n    Y = jnp.array(data[\\\"responses\\\"], dtype=jnp.float32)\\n    e = jnp.array(e_values, dtype=jnp.float32)\\n    kernel = NUTS(_gmfrm_model_v3, target_accept_prob=0.95)\\n    mcmc = MCMC(kernel, num_warmup=2000, num_samples=4000, num_chains=4,\\n                progress_bar=False)\\n    mcmc.run(jax.random.PRNGKey(cfg[\\\"seed\\\"]),\\n             Y=Y, e=e, N=cfg[\\\"N\\\"], I=cfg[\\\"I\\\"], J=cfg[\\\"J\\\"])\\n    samples = mcmc.get_samples()\\n    theta_hat = np.array(samples[\\\"theta\\\"].mean(axis=0))\\n    c_samples = np.array(samples[\\\"c\\\"])  # (total_samples, N)\\n    max_rhat, min_ess = _mcmc_diagnostics(mcmc)\\n    return {\\n        \\\"theta_hat\\\": theta_hat,\\n        \\\"c_samples\\\": c_samples,\\n        \\\"c_hat\\\": c_samples.mean(axis=0),\\n        \\\"r_hat\\\": max_rhat,\\n        \\\"ess\\\": min_ess,\\n    }\\n\\n\\n# ═══════════════════════ CSV helpers ═══════════════════════\\n\\ndef _append_csv(path, rows):\\n    df = pd.DataFrame(rows)\\n    if os.path.exists(path):\\n        df.to_csv(path, mode=\\\"a\\\", header=False, index=False)\\n    else:\\n        df.to_csv(path, index=False)\\n\\n\\ndef _dedup_csv(path, key_cols):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Remove duplicate rows from checkpoint CSV.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    if not os.path.exists(path):\\n        return\\n    df = pd.read_csv(path)\\n    n_before = len(df)\\n    df = df.d\\n\\n... [截断: 输出过长，省略 6570 字符] ...\\n\\n    rate = (i + 1) / max(elapsed, 0.1) if i > 0 else 0\\n        eta = (len(todo) - i - 1) / rate if rate > 0 else 0\\n        print(f\\\"\\\\r  [{len(done) + i + 1}/{n_seeds}] seed={seed:3d}\\\"\\n              f\\\" | {elapsed:.0f}s | ETA {eta:.0f}s   \\\", end=\\\"\\\", flush=True)\\n\\n        data = generate_exp009_data(rho=RHO_B, seed=seed)\\n        c_true = data[\\\"true_params\\\"][\\\"c\\\"]\\n        e_vals = data[\\\"true_params\\\"][\\\"e\\\"]\\n        N = data[\\\"config\\\"][\\\"N\\\"]\\n\\n        res = fit_joint_gmfrm_v3_full(data, e_vals)\\n        c_samp = res[\\\"c_samples\\\"]\\n        c_mean = c_samp.mean(axis=0)\\n        c_std = c_samp.std(axis=0)\\n        c_025 = np.percentile(c_samp, 2.5, axis=0)\\n        c_975 = np.percentile(c_samp, 97.5, axis=0)\\n        covered = ((c_true >= c_025) & (c_true <= c_975)).astype(int)\\n\\n        for n in range(N):\\n            buf.append({\\n                \\\"seed\\\": seed,\\n                \\\"model_idx\\\": n,\\n                \\\"c_true\\\": float(c_true[n]),\\n                \\\"c_hat_mean\\\": float(c_mean[n]),\\n                \\\"c_hat_std\\\": float(c_std[n]),\\n                \\\"c_hat_025\\\": float(c_025[n]),\\n                \\\"c_hat_975\\\": float(c_975[n]),\\n                \\\"covered\\\": int(covered[n]),\\n            })\\n\\n        # Flush after each seed\\n        _append_csv(csv_path, buf)\\n        buf.clear()\\n\\n    if buf:\\n        _append_csv(csv_path, buf)\\n        buf.clear()\\n\\n    elapsed = time.time() - t0\\n    print(f\\\"\\\\n  Part B completed in {elapsed:.0f}s\\\")\\n    _summarize_part_b()\\n    plot_cn_recovery()\\n\\n\\ndef _summarize_part_b():\\n    df = pd.read_csv(_csv_b())\\n    df[\\\"sq_err\\\"] = (df[\\\"c_true\\\"] - df[\\\"c_hat_mean\\\"]) ** 2\\n    seed_rmse = df.groupby(\\\"seed\\\")[\\\"sq_err\\\"].mean().apply(np.sqrt)\\n    seed_cov = df.groupby(\\\"seed\\\")[\\\"covered\\\"].mean()\\n    corr = np.corrcoef(df[\\\"c_true\\\"], df[\\\"c_hat_mean\\\"])[0, 1]\\n    n_seeds = df[\\\"seed\\\"].nunique()\\n    print(f\\\"\\\\n  c_n Recovery Summary ({n_seeds} seeds):\\\")\\n    print(f\\\"    RMSE:     {seed_rmse.mean():.4f} +/- {seed_rmse.std():.4f}\\\")\\n    print(f\\\"    Coverage: {seed_cov.mean():.4f} +/- {seed_cov.std():.4f}\\\")\\n    print(f\\\"    Corr:     {corr:.4f}\\\")\\n\\n\\ndef plot_cn_recovery():\\n    csv_path = _csv_b()\\n    png_path = _png_b()\\n    df = pd.read_csv(csv_path)\\n\\n    fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(7, 7))\\n\\n    # Error bars (very faint for dense scatter)\\n    ax.errorbar(\\n        df[\\\"c_true\\\"], df[\\\"c_hat_mean\\\"],\\n        yerr=[df[\\\"c_hat_mean\\\"] - df[\\\"c_hat_025\\\"],\\n              df[\\\"c_hat_975\\\"] - df[\\\"c_hat_mean\\\"]],\\n        fmt=\\\"none\\\", ecolor=\\\"lightblue\\\", alpha=0.04, zorder=1,\\n    )\\n    ax.scatter(df[\\\"c_true\\\"], df[\\\"c_hat_mean\\\"],\\n               s=5, alpha=0.15, color=\\\"steelblue\\\", zorder=2)\\n\\n    hi = max(df[\\\"c_true\\\"].max(), df[\\\"c_hat_975\\\"].max()) * 1.05\\n    ax.plot([0, hi], [0, hi], \\\"k--\\\", alpha=0.5, label=\\\"identity\\\")\\n\\n    rmse = np.sqrt(((df[\\\"c_true\\\"] - df[\\\"c_hat_mean\\\"]) ** 2).mean())\\n    corr = np.corrcoef(df[\\\"c_true\\\"], df[\\\"c_hat_mean\\\"])[0, 1]\\n    cov = df[\\\"covered\\\"].mean()\\n\\n    ax.set_xlabel(r\\\"$c_n$ (true)\\\", fontsize=12)\\n    ax.set_ylabel(r\\\"$\\\\hat{c}_n$ (posterior mean)\\\", fontsize=12)\\n    ax.set_title(f\\\"$c_n$ Recovery ($\\\\\\\\rho$={RHO_B})\\\\n\\\"\\n                 f\\\"RMSE={rmse:.3f}  r={corr:.3f}  95% CI cov={cov:.3f}\\\")\\n    ax.legend()\\n    ax.set_xlim(0, hi)\\n    ax.set_ylim(0, hi)\\n    ax.set_aspect(\\\"equal\\\")\\n\\n    fig.tight_layout()\\n    fig.savefig(png_path, dpi=150)\\n    plt.close(fig)\\n    print(f\\\"  Plot saved: {png_path}\\\")\\n\\n\\n# ═══════════════════════ Main ═══════════════════════\\n\\ndef main():\\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(\\n        description=\\\"exp-009: Confounding formal observation\\\")\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--part\\\", choices=[\\\"a\\\", \\\"b\\\", \\\"both\\\"], default=\\\"both\\\")\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--seeds-a\\\", type=int, default=N_SEEDS_A)\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--seeds-b\\\", type=int, default=N_SEEDS_B)\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--rhos\\\", type=float, nargs=\\\"+\\\", default=None)\\n    args = parser.parse_args()\\n\\n    if args.part in (\\\"a\\\", \\\"both\\\"):\\n        run_part_a(n_seeds=args.seeds_a, rhos=args.rhos)\\n    if args.part in (\\\"b\\\", \\\"both\\\"):\\n        run_part_b(n_seeds=args.seeds_b)\\n\\n\\nif __name__ == \\\"__main__\\\":\\n    main()\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.5}}"}}}

\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"exp-009: Confounding Formal Observation\\\\n\\\\nPart A: OVB Monte Carlo — measures sequential estimator bias as rho varies.\\\\n        rho in {0, 0.3, 0.6, 0.9} x 50 seeds, records signed bias + RMSE.\\\\nPart B: c_n ground-truth recovery — 95% credible interval coverage under\\\\n        joint G-MFRM at rho=0.6, 50 seeds.\\\\n\\\\nInputs:  ../mvp/{models.py, models_v3.py}\\\\nOutputs: bias_vs_rho.csv, bias_vs_rho.png, cn_recovery.csv, cn_recovery_scatter.png\\\\n\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n\\\\nimport os\\\\n\\\\nos.environ[\\\\\\\"XLA_FLAGS\\\\\\\"] = \\\\\\\"--xla_force_host_platform_device_count=4\\\\\\\"\\\\n\\\\nimport sys\\\\nimport time\\\\nimport argparse\\\\n\\\\nimport numpy as np\\\\nimport pandas as pd\\\\nimport matplotlib\\\\nmatplotlib.use(\\\\\\\"Agg\\\\\\\")\\\\nimport matplotlib.pyplot as plt\\\\nfrom scipy.stats import kendalltau\\\\nfrom scipy.special import expit\\\\n\\\\nsys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)), \\\\\\\"..\\\\\\\", \\\\\\\"mvp\\\\\\\"))\\\\n\\\\nimport jax\\\\nimport jax.numpy as jnp\\\\nimport numpyro\\\\nimport numpyro.distributions as dist\\\\nfrom numpyro.infer import MCMC, NUTS\\\\n\\\\nfrom models_v3 import fit_sequential_v3, fit_joint_gmfrm_v3, _gmfrm_model_v3\\\\nfrom models import _mcmc_diagnostics\\\\n\\\\nOUTPUT_DIR = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))\\\\n\\\\n\\\\n# ═══════════════════════ DGP (MVP-002 with parameterized rho) ═══════════════════════\\\\n\\\\ndef generate_exp009_data(N=50, I=100, J=8, rho=0.3, seed=42):\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"MVP-002 DGP with variable rho.\\\\n\\\\n    c ~ HalfNormal(1.5), bimodal e (30% high / 70% low),\\\\n    judge sensitivity s ~ N(0, 0.8) correlated with -d at strength rho.\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)\\\\n\\\\n    theta = rng.standard_normal(N)\\\\n    b = rng.standard_normal(I)\\\\n    d = rng.normal(0, 0.5, size=J)\\\\n    c = np.abs(rng.normal(0, 1.5, size=N))\\\\n\\\\n    # Bimodal exposure: 30% high, 70% low\\\\n    n_high = int(I * 0.3)\\\\n    e = np.zeros(I)\\\\n    idx = rng.permutation(I)\\\\n    e[idx[:n_high]] = rng.uniform(0.7, 1.0, size=n_high)\\\\n    e[idx[n_high:]] = rng.uniform(0.0, 0.2, size=I - n_high)\\\\n\\\\n    # Judge contamination sensitivity correlated with -d_j\\\\n    d_std = d.std()\\\\n    if d_std < 1e-8:\\\\n        d_std = 1.0\\\\n    z_s = rng.standard_normal(J)\\\\n    s = 0.8 * (-rho * (d / d_std) + np.sqrt(1 - rho ** 2) * z_s)\\\\n\\\\n    contamination = c[:, None, None] * e[None, :, None] * (1.0 + s[None, None, :])\\\\n    logits = theta[:, None, None] - b[None, :, None] - d[None, None, :] + contamination\\\\n    probs = expit(logits)\\\\n    responses = rng.binomial(1, probs).astype(np.int8)\\\\n\\\\n    return {\\\\n        \\\\\\\"responses\\\\\\\": responses,\\\\n        \\\\\\\"true_params\\\\\\\": {\\\\\\\"theta\\\\\\\": theta, \\\\\\\"b\\\\\\\": b, \\\\\\\"d\\\\\\\": d, \\\\\\\"c\\\\\\\": c, \\\\\\\"e\\\\\\\": e, \\\\\\\"s\\\\\\\": s},\\\\n        \\\\\\\"config\\\\\\\": {\\\\\\\"N\\\\\\\": N, \\\\\\\"I\\\\\\\": I, \\\\\\\"J\\\\\\\": J, \\\\\\\"rho\\\\\\\": rho, \\\\\\\"seed\\\\\\\": seed},\\\\n    }\\\\n\\\\n\\\\n# ═══════════════════════ Joint G-MFRM returning full c posterior ═══════════════════════\\\\n\\\\ndef fit_joint_gmfrm_v3_full(data, e_values):\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"Like fit_joint_gmfrm_v3 but returns full c posterior samples for CI.\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n    cfg = data[\\\\\\\"config\\\\\\\"]\\\\n    Y = jnp.array(data[\\\\\\\"responses\\\\\\\"], dtype=jnp.float32)\\\\n    e = jnp.array(e_values, dtype=jnp.float32)\\\\n    kernel = NUTS(_gmfrm_model_v3, target_accept_prob=0.95)\\\\n    mcmc = MCMC(kernel, num_warmup=2000, num_samples=4000, num_chains=4,\\\\n                progress_bar=False)\\\\n    mcmc.run(jax.random.PRNGKey(cfg[\\\\\\\"seed\\\\\\\"]),\\\\n             Y=Y, e=e, N=cfg[\\\\\\\"N\\\\\\\"], I=cfg[\\\\\\\"I\\\\\\\"], J=cfg[\\\\\\\"J\\\\\\\"])\\\\n    samples = mcmc.get_samples()\\\\n    theta_hat = np.array(samples[\\\\\\\"theta\\\\\\\"].mean(axis=0))\\\\n    c_samples = np.array(samples[\\\\\\\"c\\\\\\\"])  # (total_samples, N)\\\\n    max_rhat, min_ess = _mcmc_diagnostics(mcmc)\\\\n    return {\\\\n        \\\\\\\"theta_hat\\\\\\\": theta_hat,\\\\n        \\\\\\\"c_samples\\\\\\\": c_samples,\\\\n        \\\\\\\"c_hat\\\\\\\": c_samples.mean(axis=0),\\\\n        \\\\\\\"r_hat\\\\\\\": max_rhat,\\\\n        \\\\\\\"ess\\\\\\\": min_ess,\\\\n    }\\\\n\\\\n\\\\n# ═══════════════════════ CSV helpers ═══════════════════════\\\\n\\\\ndef _append_csv(path, rows):\\\\n    df = pd.DataFrame(rows)\\\\n    if os.path.exists(path):\\\\n        df.to_csv(path, mode=\\\\\\\"a\\\\\\\", header=False, index=False)\\\\n    else:\\\\n        df.to_csv(path, index=False)\\\\n\\\\n\\\\ndef _dedup_csv(path, key_cols):\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"Remove duplicate rows from checkpoint CSV.\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n    if not os.path.exists(path):\\\\n        return\\\\n    df = pd.read_csv(path)\\\\n    n_before = len(df)\\\\n    df = df.drop_duplicates(subset=key_cols, keep=\\\\\\\"last\\\\\\\")\\\\n    if len(df) < n_before:\\\\n        df.to_csv(path, index=False)\\\\n        print(f\\\\\\\"  Deduped {path}: {n_before} -> {len(df)} rows\\\\\\\")\\\\n\\\\n\\\\n# ═══════════════════════ Part A: OVB Monte Carlo ═══════════════════════\\\\n\\\\nRHOS_A = [0.0, 0.3, 0.6, 0.9]\\\\nN_SEEDS_A = 50\\\\n\\\\ndef _csv_a():\\\\n    return os.path.join(OUTPUT_DIR, {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-6","id":"msg_01AQRxzUL9BdzTRsn1qt8vuJ","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01UWt3pAFxWRp6PctbQGMPUX","name":"mcp__agent-ml-research__ssh_execute","input":{"server":"autodl-8gpu","command":"cat > /root/gated_mfrm_judge_bias/exp009/run_parallel.py << 'PYEOF'\n\"\"\"Parallel runner for exp009 Part A missing seeds.\n\nUses multiprocessing to run multiple rho-seed pairs concurrently.\nEach worker uses 1 JAX CPU device (no chain parallelism, but many workers).\n\"\"\"\nimport os\nos.environ[\"JAX_PLATFORMS\"] = \"cpu\"\nos.environ[\"CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES\"] = \"\"\nos.environ[\"XLA_FLAGS\"] = \"--xla_force_host_platform_device_count=1\"\n\nimport sys\nimport time\nimport json\nimport numpy as np\nimport pandas as pd\nfrom scipy.stats import kendalltau\nfrom concurrent.futures import ProcessPoolExecutor, as_completed\n\nsys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)), \"..\", \"mvp\"))\n\ndef _completed_pairs():\n    csv_path = os.path.join(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)), \"bias_vs_rho.csv\")\n    if not os.path.exists(csv_path):\n        return set()\n    df = pd.read_csv(csv_path)\n    done = set()\n    for (rho, seed), g in df.groupby([\"rho\", \"seed\"]):\n        if len(g) >= 2:\n            done.add((round(float(rho), 2), int(seed)))\n    return done\n\ndef run_one_seed(rho, seed):\n    \"\"\"Run both methods for one (rho, seed) pair.\"\"\"\n    os.environ[\"JAX_PLATFORMS\"] = \"cpu\"\n    os.environ[\"CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES\"] = \"\"\n    os.environ[\"XLA_FLAGS\"] = \"--xla_force_host_platform_device_count=1\"\n\n    from run_exp009 import generate_exp009_data\n\n    import jax\n    import jax.numpy as jnp\n    import numpyro\n    from numpyro.infer import MCMC, NUTS\n\n    sys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)), \"..\", \"mvp\"))\n    from models_v3 import fit_sequential_v3, fit_joint_gmfrm_v3\n    from models import _mcmc_diagnostics\n\n    data = generate_exp009_data(rho=rho, seed=seed)\n    theta_true_c = data[\"true_params\"][\"theta\"]\n    theta_true_c = theta_true_c - theta_true_c.mean()\n    e_vals = data[\"true_params\"][\"e\"]\n    c_true = data[\"true_params\"][\"c\"]\n\n    results = []\n    for method_name, fit_fn in [(\"sequential\", fit_sequential_v3),\n                                 (\"joint\", fit_joint_gmfrm_v3)]:\n        res = fit_fn(data, e_vals)\n        diff = res[\"theta_hat\"] - theta_true_c\n        tau, _ = kendalltau(res[\"theta_hat\"], theta_true_c)\n        bias_slope = float(np.polyfit(c_true, diff, 1)[0])\n        bias_corr = float(np.corrcoef(diff, c_true)[0, 1])\n        results.append({\n            \"rho\": rho,\n            \"seed\": seed,\n            \"method\": method_name,\n            \"rmse\": float(np.sqrt((diff ** 2).mean())),\n            \"mean_bias\": float(diff.mean()),\n            \"kendall_tau\": float(tau),\n            \"bias_slope\": bias_slope,\n            \"bias_corr\": bias_corr,\n        })\n    return results\n\ndef main():\n    done = _completed_pairs()\n    rhos = [0.0, 0.3, 0.6, 0.9]\n    n_seeds = 50\n    todo = [(rho, seed) for rho in rhos for seed in range(n_seeds)\n            if (round(rho, 2), seed) not in done]\n\n    print(f\"Done: {len(done)}, Todo: {len(todo)}\")\n    if not todo:\n        print(\"Nothing to do.\")\n        return\n\n    csv_path = os.path.join(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)), \"bias_vs_rho.csv\")\n    tmp_csv = csv_path + \".parallel_tmp\"\n\n    # Use 16 workers (each uses ~12 threads for JAX ops)\n    n_workers = min(16, len(todo))\n    print(f\"Starting {n_workers} workers for {len(todo)} tasks...\")\n\n    all_results = []\n    t0 = time.time()\n    completed = 0\n\n    with ProcessPoolExecutor(max_workers=n_workers) as executor:\n        futures = {executor.submit(run_one_seed, rho, seed): (rho, seed)\n                   for rho, seed in todo}\n        for future in as_completed(futures):\n            rho, seed = futures[future]\n            try:\n                rows = future.result()\n                all_results.extend(rows)\n                completed += 1\n                elapsed = time.time() - t0\n                rate = completed / elapsed if elapsed > 0 else 0\n                eta = (len(todo) - completed) / rate if rate > 0 else 0\n                print(f\"\\r  [{completed}/{len(todo)}] rho={rho:.1f} seed={seed} \"\n                      f\"| {elapsed:.0f}s | ETA {eta:.0f}s   \", end=\"\", flush=True)\n            except Exception as e:\n                print(f\"\\n  FAILED rho={rho} seed={seed}: {e}\")\n\n    print(f\"\\n\\nCompleted {completed}/{len(todo)} in {time.time()-t0:.0f}s\")\n\n    if all_results:\n        new_df = pd.DataFrame(all_results)\n        if os.path.exists(csv_path):\n            old_df = pd.read_csv(csv_path)\n            merged = pd.concat([old_df, new_df], ignore_index=True)\n            merged = merged.drop_duplicates(subset=[\"rho\", \"seed\", \"method\"], keep=\"last\")\n        else:\n            merged = new_df\n        merged = merged.sort_values([\"rho\", \"seed\", \"method\"]).reset_index(drop=True)\n        merged.to_csv(csv_path, index=False)\n        print(f\"Saved {len(merged)} rows to {csv_path}\")\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    main()\nPYEOF\necho \"Script written\""},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1064,"cache_read_input_tokens":59857,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":1064},"output_tokens":61,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"f3eb0a2e-f434-4dd6-84a2-48b7137b2243","uuid":"71be3e70-d1e3-4eb1-90b8-ef64d1f0fd93"}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-6","id":"msg_01WBHwJo7KJRt2r9K9PMpDAT","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01JEN3oQoktA2dEfwe9aEVLf","name":"mcp__agent-ml-research__ssh_execute","input":{"server":"autodl-8gpu","command":"cat > /root/gated_mfrm_judge_bias/exp009/run_parallel.py << 'PYEOF'\n\"\"\"Parallel runner for exp009 Part A missing seeds.\n\nUses multiprocessing (spawn) to run multiple rho-seed pairs concurrently.\nEach worker gets limited CPU threads for efficient parallelism.\n\"\"\"\nimport os, sys, time, json\nimport multiprocessing as mp\n\ndef _worker_init():\n    os.environ[\"JAX_PLATFORMS\"] = \"cpu\"\n    os.environ[\"CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES\"] = \"\"\n    os.environ[\"XLA_FLAGS\"] = \"--xla_force_host_platform_device_count=1\"\n    os.environ[\"OMP_NUM_THREADS\"] = \"26\"\n    os.environ[\"MKL_NUM_THREADS\"] = \"26\"\n    os.environ[\"OPENBLAS_NUM_THREADS\"] = \"26\"\n\ndef run_one_seed(args):\n    rho, seed = args\n    _worker_init()\n    \n    import numpy as np\n    from scipy.stats import kendalltau\n    \n    sys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)), \"..\", \"mvp\"))\n    sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))\n    \n    from run_exp009 import generate_exp009_data\n    from models_v3 import fit_sequential_v3, fit_joint_gmfrm_v3\n    \n    data = generate_exp009_data(rho=rho, seed=seed)\n    theta_true_c = data[\"true_params\"][\"theta\"]\n    theta_true_c = theta_true_c - theta_true_c.mean()\n    e_vals = data[\"true_params\"][\"e\"]\n    c_true = data[\"true_params\"][\"c\"]\n\n    results = []\n    for method_name, fit_fn in [(\"sequential\", fit_sequential_v3),\n                                 (\"joint\", fit_joint_gmfrm_v3)]:\n        res = fit_fn(data, e_vals)\n        diff = res[\"theta_hat\"] - theta_true_c\n        tau, _ = kendalltau(res[\"theta_hat\"], theta_true_c)\n        bias_slope = float(np.polyfit(c_true, diff, 1)[0])\n        bias_corr = float(np.corrcoef(diff, c_true)[0, 1])\n        results.append({\n            \"rho\": rho, \"seed\": seed, \"method\": method_name,\n            \"rmse\": float(np.sqrt((diff ** 2).mean())),\n            \"mean_bias\": float(diff.mean()),\n            \"kendall_tau\": float(tau),\n            \"bias_slope\": bias_slope, \"bias_corr\": bias_corr,\n        })\n    return results\n\ndef main():\n    import pandas as pd\n    import numpy as np\n    \n    csv_path = os.path.join(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)), \"bias_vs_rho.csv\")\n    \n    done = set()\n    if os.path.exists(csv_path):\n        df = pd.read_csv(csv_path)\n        for (rho, seed), g in df.groupby([\"rho\", \"seed\"]):\n            if len(g) >= 2:\n                done.add((round(float(rho), 2), int(seed)))\n    \n    rhos = [0.0, 0.3, 0.6, 0.9]\n    todo = [(rho, seed) for rho in rhos for seed in range(50)\n            if (round(rho, 2), seed) not in done]\n    \n    print(f\"Done: {len(done)}, Todo: {len(todo)}\", flush=True)\n    if not todo:\n        print(\"Nothing to do.\")\n        return\n    \n    n_workers = min(8, len(todo))\n    print(f\"Starting {n_workers} spawn workers...\", flush=True)\n    \n    ctx = mp.get_context(\"spawn\")\n    all_results = []\n    t0 = time.time()\n    completed = 0\n    \n    with ctx.Pool(n_workers) as pool:\n        for rows in pool.imap_unordered(run_one_seed, todo):\n            all_results.extend(rows)\n            completed += 1\n            elapsed = time.time() - t0\n            rate = completed / elapsed if elapsed > 0 else 0\n            eta = (len(todo) - completed) / rate if rate > 0 else 0\n            rho, seed = rows[0][\"rho\"], rows[0][\"seed\"]\n            print(f\"  [{completed}/{len(todo)}] rho={rho:.1f} seed={seed} \"\n                  f\"| {elapsed:.0f}s | ETA {eta:.0f}s\", flush=True)\n    \n    print(f\"\\nCompleted {completed}/{len(todo)} in {time.time()-t0:.0f}s\", flush=True)\n    \n    if all_results:\n        new_df = pd.DataFrame(all_results)\n        if os.path.exists(csv_path):\n            old_df = pd.read_csv(csv_path)\n            merged = pd.concat([old_df, new_df], ignore_index=True)\n            merged = merged.drop_duplicates(subset=[\"rho\", \"seed\", \"method\"], keep=\"last\")\n        else:\n            merged = new_df\n        merged = merged.sort_values([\"rho\", \"seed\", \"method\"]).reset_index(drop=True)\n        merged.to_csv(csv_path, index=False)\n        print(f\"Saved {len(merged)} rows to {csv_path}\", flush=True)\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    main()\nPYEOF\necho \"Script written\""},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":623,"cache_read_input_tokens":64478,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":623},"output_tokens":8,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"f3eb0a2e-f434-4dd6-84a2-48b7137b2243","uuid":"1ae30880-ef77-4f5a-a53e-f51436dff2fc"}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_0126s5xXodUugb96p2kwdsBC","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\\section{Confounding in LLM Evaluation}\n2\t\\label{sec:confounding}\n3\t\n4\t\\begin{figure*}[t]\n5\t\\centering\n6\t\\begin{minipage}[t]{0.45\\textwidth}\n7\t\\centering\n8\t\\includegraphics[width=\\textwidth]{fig2_ovb_bias}\n9\t\\end{minipage}\n10\t\\hfill\n11\t\\begin{minipage}[t]{0.45\\textwidth}\n12\t\\centering\n13\t\\includegraphics[width=\\textwidth]{fig4_bias_vs_rho}\n14\t\\end{minipage}\n15\t\\caption{(a)~Sequential estimation bias ($\\hat{\\theta}_{\\mathrm{seq}} - \\theta_{\\mathrm{true}}$) versus true contamination susceptibility $c_n$. Pearson $r = 0.86$ ($p < 0.001$) confirms systematic overestimation of contaminated models. (b)~Sequential estimator bias remains approximately constant across confounding levels ($\\rho$), confirming omission-driven rather than confounding-strength-dependent bias. Joint G-MFRM estimation reduces bias by approximately 70\\% at all $\\rho$ levels. Synthetic data: $N = 50$, $I = 100$, $J = 8$.}\n16\t\\label{fig:ovb_bias}\n17\t\\end{figure*}\n18\t\n19\tThe Many-Facet Rasch Model (MFRM) extends classical Rasch measurement to settings with multiple rater facets \\citep{linacre1989mfrm, linacre1994mfrm}. Originating in educational measurement, the model treats rater variation as a nuisance facet to be estimated and removed rather than a source of noise to be averaged over. In the LLM evaluation context, where $J$ judges score $N$ models across $I$ items, the MFRM specifies\n20\t%\n21\t\\begin{equation}\n22\t\\label{eq:mfrm}\n23\t\\log \\frac{P(Y_{nij} \\geq k \\mid \\theta_n, b_i, d_j, \\tau_k)}{P(Y_{nij} < k \\mid \\theta_n, b_i, d_j, \\tau_k)} = \\theta_n - b_i - d_j - \\tau_k,\n24\t\\end{equation}\n25\t%\n26\twhere $Y_{nij}$ is the ordinal rating by judge $j$ for model $n$ on item $i$, $\\theta_n$ is the ability of model $n$ ($n = 1, \\ldots, N$), $b_i$ is the difficulty of item $i$ ($i = 1, \\ldots, I$), $d_j$ is the severity of judge $j$ ($j = 1, \\ldots, J$), and $\\tau_k$ is the threshold for category $k$. All facets reside on a common logit scale, so model ability is directly comparable after adjustment for item difficulty and judge severity. \\citet{jiao2025mfrm_llm} applied this framework to compare ten LLM raters with human experts, showing that MFRM separates genuine ability from systematic judge effects in multi-judge panels.\n27\t\n28\tHowever, standard MFRM assumes that all systematic sources of score variation are captured by the modeled facets. This assumption breaks down when some models have been exposed to benchmark items during training \\citep{dcr2025}. Such contamination inflates scores on exposed items through memorization rather than genuine ability. We represent this effect as an interaction $c_n \\cdot e_i$, where $c_n \\geq 0$ is the contamination susceptibility of model $n$ and $e_i \\in [0, 1]$ is the exposure level of item $i$. The complete data-generating process becomes\n29\t%\n30\t\\begin{equation}\n31\t\\label{eq:full}\n32\t\\eta_{nijk} = \\theta_n - b_i - d_j - \\tau_k + c_n \\cdot e_i.\n33\t\\end{equation}\n34\t\n35\t\n36\tWhen a practitioner fits the standard MFRM (Eq.~\\ref{eq:mfrm}) to data generated by Eq.~\\ref{eq:full}, the contamination term is absent from the estimated model. Its influence does not vanish but is absorbed into the estimated parameters $\\tilde{\\theta}_n$, $\\tilde{b}_i$, and $\\tilde{d}_j$, which distorts all downstream inferences.\n37\t\n38\tThe consequence of this omission can be stated precisely.\n39\t\n40\t\\begin{observation}[Omitted Variable Bias in MFRM]\n41\t\\label{obs:ovb}\n42\tLet the full model be $\\eta_{nijk} = \\theta_n - b_i - d_j - \\tau_k + c_n \\cdot e_i$ and the short model omit the contamination term: $\\tilde{\\eta}_{nijk} = \\tilde{\\theta}_n - \\tilde{b}_i - \\tilde{d}_j - \\tau_k$. Under a linear approximation to the ordinal logit link \\citep{wooldridge2010, angrist2009mostly},\\footnote{The OVB formula is exact for linear models. We apply it to the MFRM linear predictor $\\eta$, which is an approximation. Synthetic validation confirms the approximation quality: the Pearson correlation between the predicted bias direction and empirical bias reaches $r = 0.86$ ($p < 0.001$), indicating that the linear result accurately characterizes bias direction and relative magnitude.}\n43\t\\begin{equation}\n44\t\\label{eq:ovb}\n45\t\\mathrm{plim}(\\tilde{\\theta}_n - \\theta_n) = \\gamma_n \\cdot c_n,\n46\t\\end{equation}\n47\twhere $\\gamma_n$ depends on the projection of the exposure vector $(e_i)$ onto the design matrix of the short model (explicit form in Appendix~\\ref{app:gamma}). The bias is non-zero whenever $\\mathrm{Cov}(d_j,\\, c_n \\cdot e_i) \\neq 0$. This captures the confounding component; in the nonlinear MFRM, a specification bias from omitting $c_n \\cdot e_i$ persists even at zero covariance (Section~\\ref{sec:experiments}).\n48\t\\end{observation}\n49\t\n50\tG-MFRM's Stage~3 inference (Eq.~\\ref{eq:gmfrm}) uses the full nonlinear ordinal logistic likelihood; the linear approximation serves only as directional motivation. Monte Carlo simulation confirms that bias magnitude is approximately constant across confounding levels $\\rho$ (Figure~\\ref{fig:ovb_bias}b), indicating that omission of $c_n \\cdot e_i$---not its correlation with $d_j$---is the primary driver. Neither sequential ordering resolves this: bias-first approaches \\citep{cyclicjudge2026} absorb contamination into $\\hat{\\theta}$, while contamination-first approaches \\citep{dcr2025} misattribute judge severity as contamination. Synthetic validation ($N = 50$, $I = 100$, $J = 8$, 20 seeds) confirms directional bias ($r = 0.86$, $p < 0.001$; Figure~\\ref{fig:ovb_bias}a)."}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"f3eb0a2e-f434-4dd6-84a2-48b7137b2243","uuid":"bc6ddba9-0333-42c1-978b-077bb0adb29f","timestamp":"2026-05-25T12:02:21.686Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-prod43/projects/gated_mfrm_judge_bias/docs/paper/background.tex","content":"\\section{Confounding in LLM Evaluation}\n\\label{sec:confounding}\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\begin{minipage}[t]{0.45\\textwidth}\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\textwidth]{fig2_ovb_bias}\n\\end{minipage}\n\\hfill\n\\begin{minipage}[t]{0.45\\textwidth}\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\textwidth]{fig4_bias_vs_rho}\n\\end{minipage}\n\\caption{(a)~Sequential estimation bias ($\\hat{\\theta}_{\\mathrm{seq}} - \\theta_{\\mathrm{true}}$) versus true contamination susceptibility $c_n$. Pearson $r = 0.86$ ($p < 0.001$) confirms systematic overestimation of contaminated models. (b)~Sequential estimator bias remains approximately constant across confounding levels ($\\rho$), confirming omission-driven rather than confounding-strength-dependent bias. Joint G-MFRM estimation reduces bias by approximately 70\\% at all $\\rho$ levels. Synthetic data: $N = 50$, $I = 100$, $J = 8$.}\n\\label{fig:ovb_bias}\n\\end{figure*}\n\nThe Many-Facet Rasch Model (MFRM) extends classical Rasch measurement to settings with multiple rater facets \\citep{linacre1989mfrm, linacre1994mfrm}. Originating in educational measurement, the model treats rater variation as a nuisance facet to be estimated and removed rather than a source of noise to be averaged over. In the LLM evaluation context, where $J$ judges score $N$ models across $I$ items, the MFRM specifies\n%\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:mfrm}\n\\log \\frac{P(Y_{nij} \\geq k \\mid \\theta_n, b_i, d_j, \\tau_k)}{P(Y_{nij} < k \\mid \\theta_n, b_i, d_j, \\tau_k)} = \\theta_n - b_i - d_j - \\tau_k,\n\\end{equation}\n%\nwhere $Y_{nij}$ is the ordinal rating by judge $j$ for model $n$ on item $i$, $\\theta_n$ is the ability of model $n$ ($n = 1, \\ldots, N$), $b_i$ is the difficulty of item $i$ ($i = 1, \\ldots, I$), $d_j$ is the severity of judge $j$ ($j = 1, \\ldots, J$), and $\\tau_k$ is the threshold for category $k$. All facets reside on a common logit scale, so model ability is directly comparable after adjustment for item difficulty and judge severity. \\citet{jiao2025mfrm_llm} applied this framework to compare ten LLM raters with human experts, showing that MFRM separates genuine ability from systematic judge effects in multi-judge panels.\n\nHowever, standard MFRM assumes that all systematic sources of score variation are captured by the modeled facets. This assumption breaks down when some models have been exposed to benchmark items during training \\citep{dcr2025}. Such contamination inflates scores on exposed items through memorization rather than genuine ability. We represent this effect as an interaction $c_n \\cdot e_i$, where $c_n \\geq 0$ is the contamination susceptibility of model $n$ and $e_i \\in [0, 1]$ is the exposure level of item $i$. The complete data-generating process becomes\n%\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:full}\n\\eta_{nijk} = \\theta_n - b_i - d_j - \\tau_k + c_n \\cdot e_i.\n\\end{equation}\n\n\nWhen a practitioner fits the standard MFRM (Eq.~\\ref{eq:mfrm}) to data generated by Eq.~\\ref{eq:full}, the contamination term is absent from the estimated model. Its influence does not vanish but is absorbed into the estimated parameters $\\tilde{\\theta}_n$, $\\tilde{b}_i$, and $\\tilde{d}_j$, which distorts all downstream inferences.\n\nThe consequence of this omission can be stated precisely.\n\n\\begin{observation}[Omitted Variable Bias in MFRM]\n\\label{obs:ovb}\nLet the full model be $\\eta_{nijk} = \\theta_n - b_i - d_j - \\tau_k + c_n \\cdot e_i$ and the short model omit the contamination term: $\\tilde{\\eta}_{nijk} = \\tilde{\\theta}_n - \\tilde{b}_i - \\tilde{d}_j - \\tau_k$. Under a linear approximation to the ordinal logit link \\citep{wooldridge2010, angrist2009mostly},\\footnote{The OVB formula is exact for linear models. We apply it to the MFRM linear predictor $\\eta$, which is an approximation. Synthetic validation confirms the approximation quality: the Pearson correlation between the predicted bias direction and empirical bias reaches $r = 0.86$ ($p < 0.001$), indicating that the linear result accurately characterizes bias direction and relative magnitude.}\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:ovb}\n\\mathrm{plim}(\\tilde{\\theta}_n - \\theta_n) = \\gamma_n \\cdot c_n,\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\gamma_n$ depends on the projection of the exposure vector $(e_i)$ onto the design matrix of the short model (explicit form in Appendix~\\ref{app:gamma}). The bias is non-zero whenever $\\mathrm{Cov}(d_j,\\, c_n \\cdot e_i) \\neq 0$. This captures the confounding component; in the nonlinear MFRM, a specification bias from omitting $c_n \\cdot e_i$ persists even at zero covariance (Section~\\ref{sec:experiments}).\n\\end{observation}\n\nG-MFRM's Stage~3 inference (Eq.~\\ref{eq:gmfrm}) uses the full nonlinear ordinal logistic likelihood; the linear approximation serves only as directional motivation. Monte Carlo simulation confirms that bias magnitude is approximately constant across confounding levels $\\rho$ (Figure~\\ref{fig:ovb_bias}b), indicating that omission of $c_n \\cdot e_i$---not its correlation with $d_j$---is the primary driver. Neither sequential ordering resolves this: bias-first approaches \\citep{cyclicjudge2026} absorb contamination into $\\hat{\\theta}$, while contamination-first approaches \\citep{dcr2025} misattribute judge severity as contamination. Synthetic validation ($N = 50$, $I = 100$, $J = 8$, 20 seeds) confirms directional bias ($r = 0.86$, $p < 0.001$; Figure~\\ref{fig:ovb_bias}a).","numLines":50,"startLine":1,"totalLines":50}}}
